People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. That sounds strange, maybe, but… yes, it’s been there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to experience pleasure from things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, at least somewhat. I usually really love painting, and I like wandering through museum exhibits, but lately I just leave my supplies sitting there. And even when I think, “Oh, I should go see something,” I can’t really make myself care enough to go. I don’t know if that counts, but it does feel different from me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I’m sure everyone feels that way sometimes. But I’ve been replaying things in my head a lot—missed family plans, tense conversations with my husband—and I end up feeling like I’m letting everyone down. As a wife, as a mother… even at work as a manager. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your movement or activity level lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. I don’t think it’s anything dramatic, but… my staff has mentioned that I’m slower to answer questions in meetings lately. Like there’s this pause before I can get my thoughts organized and the words out. I hope that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but I’ve been sort of picking at meals… or I’ll realize I forgot to eat lunch at work. And my skirts are fitting a bit looser. It’s not a huge amount, I don’t think, but I did notice it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, gray feeling most days?\n\nPatient: It’s been… most days lately, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I wake up with this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it’s even really started. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I just feel tired of dealing with things, but not like I’m planning anything or wanting to hurt myself. I don’t want to alarm anyone. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week do you feel this heavy, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: Probably… most days? I mean, not every single day all day, but if I’m being honest, it’s there more often than not lately when I wake up. Maybe five or six days a week. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… about a month? Maybe four weeks or so. It started after I’d had this stretch where I was really energized and driven, and then it kind of shifted into this lower feeling. But I don’t mean it’s been awful every minute—just… persistent enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d say mild, most of the time. It’s there when I wake up—this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it starts—but I can usually still get myself going. I don’t know if that counts as mild, but it doesn’t feel severe.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty fulfilling your responsibilities at work or at home due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. I’m still showing up to work, still getting things done at home. It’s just… I’m slower with decisions at work, and I think people may notice I’m not as on top of things as usual.\n\nAt home, I’ve been less reliable with family plans—like I’ll say I’m going to do something and then I kind of drag my feet or hope it goes away. And I haven’t been doing as much with the art stuff I usually do, the things that keep me connected. So… mild, I guess, but yes, it’s affecting things some. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I wake up with this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it’s even really started. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… about a month? Maybe four weeks or so. It started after I’d had this stretch where I was really energized and driven, and then it kind of shifted into this lower feeling. But I don’t mean it’s been awful every minute—just… persistent enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d say mild, most of the time. I don’t know if that counts as mild, but it doesn’t feel severe.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty fulfilling your responsibilities at work or at home due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. And I haven’t been doing as much with the art stuff I usually do, the things that keep me connected. So… mild, I guess, but yes, it’s affecting things some. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your lack of interest in painting and other activities you used to enjoy is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d probably call it mild… maybe leaning moderate on certain days, but mostly mild. Does “mild” sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your heavy, gray feeling and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your daily life in a way that you would describe as moderate or marked?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it marked… that sounds too strong for me. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped liking everything, but… I usually really love painting, and I like wandering around museum exhibits when I get the chance. Lately my paints just kind of sit there untouched, and even when I think about going to a museum, I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually go. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if I’d call it “sad” all the time exactly, but most days lately I wake up with this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it even starts. That sounds strange, maybe, but… yes, it’s been there a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer able to experience pleasure from things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, at least somewhat. I usually really love painting, and I like wandering through museum exhibits, but lately I just leave my supplies sitting there. And even when I think, “Oh, I should go see something,” I can’t really make myself care enough to go. I don’t know if that counts, but it does feel different from me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I’m sure everyone feels that way sometimes. But I’ve been replaying things in my head a lot—missed family plans, tense conversations with my husband—and I end up feeling like I’m letting everyone down. As a wife, as a mother… even at work as a manager. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but it’s been hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your movement or activity level lately?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. I don’t think it’s anything dramatic, but… my staff has mentioned that I’m slower to answer questions in meetings lately. Like there’s this pause before I can get my thoughts organized and the words out. I hope that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I haven’t been trying to lose weight or anything, but I’ve been sort of picking at meals… or I’ll realize I forgot to eat lunch at work. And my skirts are fitting a bit looser. It’s not a huge amount, I don’t think, but I did notice it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, gray feeling most days?\n\nPatient: It’s been… most days lately, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I wake up with this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it’s even really started. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I just feel tired of dealing with things, but not like I’m planning anything or wanting to hurt myself. I don’t want to alarm anyone. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week do you feel this heavy, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: Probably… most days? I mean, not every single day all day, but if I’m being honest, it’s there more often than not lately when I wake up. Maybe five or six days a week. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… about a month? Maybe four weeks or so. It started after I’d had this stretch where I was really energized and driven, and then it kind of shifted into this lower feeling. But I don’t mean it’s been awful every minute—just… persistent enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d say mild, most of the time. It’s there when I wake up—this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it starts—but I can usually still get myself going. I don’t know if that counts as mild, but it doesn’t feel severe.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty fulfilling your responsibilities at work or at home due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. I’m still showing up to work, still getting things done at home. It’s just… I’m slower with decisions at work, and I think people may notice I’m not as on top of things as usual.\n\nAt home, I’ve been less reliable with family plans—like I’ll say I’m going to do something and then I kind of drag my feet or hope it goes away. And I haven’t been doing as much with the art stuff I usually do, the things that keep me connected. So… mild, I guess, but yes, it’s affecting things some. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your lack of interest in painting and other activities you used to enjoy is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d probably call it mild… maybe leaning moderate on certain days, but mostly mild. I mean, I usually really love painting, and I like just wandering through museum exhibits, but lately my supplies just sit there untouched. And when I think about going, I sort of… can’t make myself care enough to actually do it.\n\nI’m not completely uninterested in everything, so I don’t want to overstate it. But it’s noticeable, yes. Does “mild” sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your heavy, gray feeling and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your daily life in a way that you would describe as moderate or marked?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it marked… that sounds too strong for me. Maybe not even moderate most days, honestly. I’m still getting to work and taking care of things at home.\n\nBut it is interfering some. I’m slower making decisions at work, and at home I’ve been less reliable with family plans than I’d like. And I’ve definitely pulled back from painting and art things, which usually help me feel connected. So… mild overall, I think, with some noticeable effects. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your recent feelings and experiences as having a significant impact on your social and occupational functioning?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but I’d say it’s not huge. I’m still showing up for work, and I’m still handling things at home, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it has had an impact. At work I’m slower with decisions, and at home I’ve been a little less reliable with family plans. And socially—or maybe more personally—I’ve been pulling back from the art things that usually keep me feeling connected. So… significant in the sense that I notice it, but mild overall, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are any activities that you still find pleasure in, or has the lack of interest affected almost all activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not almost all, no… I wouldn’t say that. There are still some things I can enjoy, I think. It’s more that the things that are usually really “me,” like painting or going to museum exhibits, have taken a hit.\n\nI usually love painting, but lately the supplies are just sitting there, and I look at them and sort of… don’t feel pulled to start. Same with museums—I think about going, and then I just can’t quite make myself care enough to actually go. So it’s noticeable, but I don’t think it’s everything. Does that distinction make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I wake up with this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it’s even really started. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I just feel tired of dealing with things, but not like I’m planning anything or wanting to hurt myself. I don’t want to alarm anyone. Is that… is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week do you feel this heavy, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: Probably… most days? I mean, not every single day all day, but if I’m being honest, it’s there more often than not lately when I wake up. Maybe five or six days a week. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many weeks have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think… about a month? Maybe four weeks or so. It started after I’d had this stretch where I was really energized and driven, and then it kind of shifted into this lower feeling. But I don’t mean it’s been awful every minute—just… persistent enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d say mild, most of the time. It’s there when I wake up—this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it starts—but I can usually still get myself going. I don’t know if that counts as mild, but it doesn’t feel severe.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty fulfilling your responsibilities at work or at home due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. I’m still showing up to work, still getting things done at home. It’s just… I’m slower with decisions at work, and I think people may notice I’m not as on top of things as usual.\n\nAt home, I’ve been less reliable with family plans—like I’ll say I’m going to do something and then I kind of drag my feet or hope it goes away. And I haven’t been doing as much with the art stuff I usually do, the things that keep me connected. So… mild, I guess, but yes, it’s affecting things some. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your lack of interest in painting and other activities you used to enjoy is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d probably call it mild… maybe leaning moderate on certain days, but mostly mild. I mean, I usually really love painting, and I like just wandering through museum exhibits, but lately my supplies just sit there untouched. And when I think about going, I sort of… can’t make myself care enough to actually do it.\n\nI’m not completely uninterested in everything, so I don’t want to overstate it. But it’s noticeable, yes. Does “mild” sound right for that?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your heavy, gray feeling and lack of interest in activities are interfering with your daily life in a way that you would describe as moderate or marked?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d call it marked… that sounds too strong for me. Maybe not even moderate most days, honestly. I’m still getting to work and taking care of things at home.\n\nBut it is interfering some. I’m slower making decisions at work, and at home I’ve been less reliable with family plans than I’d like. And I’ve definitely pulled back from painting and art things, which usually help me feel connected. So… mild overall, I think, with some noticeable effects. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your recent feelings and experiences as having a significant impact on your social and occupational functioning?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but I’d say it’s not huge. I’m still showing up for work, and I’m still handling things at home, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nBut it has had an impact. At work I’m slower with decisions, and at home I’ve been a little less reliable with family plans. And socially—or maybe more personally—I’ve been pulling back from the art things that usually keep me feeling connected. So… significant in the sense that I notice it, but mild overall, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say there are any activities that you still find pleasure in, or has the lack of interest affected almost all activities you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not almost all, no… I wouldn’t say that. There are still some things I can enjoy, I think. It’s more that the things that are usually really “me,” like painting or going to museum exhibits, have taken a hit.\n\nI usually love painting, but lately the supplies are just sitting there, and I look at them and sort of… don’t feel pulled to start. Same with museums—I think about going, and then I just can’t quite make myself care enough to actually go. So it’s noticeable, but I don’t think it’s everything. Does that distinction make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you noticed a decrease in interest in painting and other activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been about a month, maybe a little less or more… but around a month that I’ve really noticed it.\n\nIt kind of came after this stretch where I was much more energized and driven than usual, and then I swung into this lower place, and the painting and museum stuff just stopped feeling as appealing. I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s been pretty persistent for these past few weeks. Does that sound like the kind of timeframe you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your recent feelings and experiences as causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, but I’d still call it mild. I don’t feel like I’m falling apart or anything. I’m still going to work, still taking care of things at home.\n\nBut it is distressing to notice I’m not quite myself. I’ve been slower making decisions at work, and with family plans I haven’t been as reliable as I’d like. And I’ve been pulling back from the art-related things that usually help me feel connected. So yes, it bothers me… I just don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’d say it’s been mostly mild, but… pretty steady, if that makes sense. Most days lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it even starts.

It doesn’t usually feel overwhelming or anything—I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is—but it’s there more often than not. Some mornings feel a little heavier than others, but I don’t think I’ve had dramatic swings where it suddenly becomes unbearable. Is that the kind of distinction you mean?

But I don’t mean it’s been awful every minute—just… persistent enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy, gray feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I’d say mild, most of the time. It’s there when I wake up—this kind of heavy, gray feeling, like I’m already disappointed in the day before it starts—but I can usually still get myself going. I don’t know if that counts as mild, but it doesn’t feel severe.\n\nDoctor: Have you had difficulty fulfilling your responsibilities at work or at home due to how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m falling apart or anything. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your lack of interest in painting and other activities you used to enjoy is mild, moderate, or severe?\n\nPatient: I’d probably call it mild… maybe leaning moderate on certain days, but mostly mild. And when I think about going, I sort of… can’t make myself care enough to actually do it.\n\nI’m not completely uninterested in everything, so I don’t want to overstate it. But it’s noticeable, yes. I’m slower making decisions at work, and at home I’ve been less reliable with family plans than I’d like. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your recent feelings and experiences as having a significant impact on your social and occupational functioning?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, but I’d say it’s not huge.
